I don't think I'm rushing, exactly... just eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating in secret during those episodes, or hiding the fact that you're eating from others?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean... I'm usually just by myself anyway when it happens. So... I don't think I'm purposely hiding anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what or how much you're eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess not really. It's just... once I get started on those nights, I kind of keep going back to the kitchen. Even while I'm telling myself to stop, I just don't. I don't really feel back in charge of it until I'm just... uncomfortably full.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. It pretty much just stays the same, I guess. I haven't really noticed any big changes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. Sometimes in the evenings, after work... especially if I've been trying to be strict during the day. I just end up eating... a lot more than I meant to. Like, I'll have dinner, and then... maybe cereal, some crackers, leftovers, sweets... all within a couple of hours, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to choose what you eat during those episodes or do you feel like you're not really making choices?\n\nPatient: Um... I think I already mentioned this, but... no, I don't really feel like I'm making choices. Like I said before, once I start, I just keep going back for more. I tell myself to stop, but I just don't. I'm not really back in charge until I'm... well, until I'm too full.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to control how much you're eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I just said... no. I don't really control the amount either. Once I get started on those nights, I just keep going back to the kitchen. Even while I'm telling myself to stop... I just don't. I don't really feel back in charge until I'm... uncomfortably full.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating more than you meant to happen?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess for the past few months, it's been happening... most weeks. Usually just one or two nights a week. I just eat way more than I meant to, and then... well, the next day I try to fix it. I'll skip meals, or... maybe stay on the treadmill a lot longer than usual. Just to make up for it, I guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don't think I'm purposely hiding anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what or how much you're eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess not really. It's just... once I get started on those nights, I kind of keep going back to the kitchen. Even while I'm telling myself to stop, I just don't. I don't really feel back in charge of it until I'm just... uncomfortably full.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. It pretty much just stays the same, I guess. I haven't really noticed any big changes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. Sometimes in the evenings, after work... especially if I've been trying to be strict during the day. I just end up eating... a lot more than I meant to. Like, I'll have dinner, and then... maybe cereal, some crackers, leftovers, sweets... all within a couple of hours, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to choose what you eat during those episodes or do you feel like you're not really making choices?\n\nPatient: Um... I think I already mentioned this, but... no, I don't really feel like I'm making choices. Like I said before, once I start, I just keep going back for more. I tell myself to stop, but I just don't. I'm not really back in charge until I'm... well, until I'm too full.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to control how much you're eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I just said... no. I don't really control the amount either. Once I get started on those nights, I just keep going back to the kitchen. Even while I'm telling myself to stop... I just don't. I don't really feel back in charge until I'm... uncomfortably full.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating more than you meant to happen?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess for the past few months, it's been happening... most weeks. Usually just one or two nights a week. I just eat way more than I meant to, and then... well, the next day I try to fix it. I'll skip meals, or... maybe stay on the treadmill a lot longer than usual. Just to make up for it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: How often do you try to make up for the extra food you ate by skipping meals or exercising more?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I just said, it's... it's about the same. If I have one of those nights, the next day is when I try to fix it. So... yeah, for the past few months, it's most weeks. One or two times a week I'll... skip eating or stay on the treadmill. Just depends on how many times I messed up the night before.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever used any methods to try to purge the food you ate during those episodes, such as vomiting or laxatives?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't... I've never done anything like that. Just the things I already mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any significant distress or impairment in social, occupational, or other important areas of functioning due to these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I don't really feel back in charge until I'm... well, until I'm uncomfortably full.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I suppose so. I don't really like to dwell on it, though. I just... try to go to sleep after.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really fast during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I'm not really sure. I haven't paid much attention to that. I don't think I'm rushing, exactly... just eating.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating in secret during those episodes, or hiding the fact that you're eating from others?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean... I'm usually just by myself anyway when it happens. So... I don't think I'm purposely hiding anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what or how much you're eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess not really. It's just... once I get started on those nights, I kind of keep going back to the kitchen. Even while I'm telling myself to stop, I just don't. I don't really feel back in charge of it until I'm just... uncomfortably full.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over time due to these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um... no, I don't think so. It pretty much just stays the same, I guess. I haven't really noticed any big changes.\n\nDoctor: Do you have episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess so. Sometimes in the evenings, after work... especially if I've been trying to be strict during the day. I just end up eating... a lot more than I meant to. Like, I'll have dinner, and then... maybe cereal, some crackers, leftovers, sweets... all within a couple of hours, I suppose.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to choose what you eat during those episodes or do you feel like you're not really making choices?\n\nPatient: Um... I think I already mentioned this, but... no, I don't really feel like I'm making choices. Like I said before, once I start, I just keep going back for more. I tell myself to stop, but I just don't. I'm not really back in charge until I'm... well, until I'm too full.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to control how much you're eating during those episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I just said... no. I don't really control the amount either. Once I get started on those nights, I just keep going back to the kitchen. Even while I'm telling myself to stop... I just don't. I don't really feel back in charge until I'm... uncomfortably full.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating more than you meant to happen?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess for the past few months, it's been happening... most weeks. Usually just one or two nights a week. I just eat way more than I meant to, and then... well, the next day I try to fix it. I'll skip meals, or... maybe stay on the treadmill a lot longer than usual. Just to make up for it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: How often do you try to make up for the extra food you ate by skipping meals or exercising more?\n\nPatient: Um... well, like I just said, it's... it's about the same. If I have one of those nights, the next day is when I try to fix it. So... yeah, for the past few months, it's most weeks. One or two times a week I'll... skip eating or stay on the treadmill. Just depends on how many times I messed up the night before.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever used any methods to try to purge the food you ate during those episodes, such as vomiting or laxatives?\n\nPatient: Um... no. I don't... I've never done anything like that. Just the things I already mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any significant distress or impairment in social, occupational, or other important areas of functioning due to these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I mean... I still go to work. I'm an insurance agent, so I have clients to keep up with, and... I still do that. And I help mentor some of the newer agents. I still do everything I need to do at home, too. It's just... sometimes, I guess... if I have a public event or something, I might feel a little... distracted. Or just... less confident, I suppose, if I'm worrying about how my clothes are fitting that day. But... I still do my job.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month would you say you have one of these episodes where you eat more than you meant to?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I think I mentioned a minute ago... it happens most weeks. Usually one or two nights a week where I eat way more than I meant to. So... I guess maybe six or eight times a month? It's been like that for the past few months. And then... yeah, like I said, the next day is when I try to make up for it by skipping a meal or exercising. I don't keep an exact count, but... that's probably close.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
